## assistant
Both curves are informative.

**P2-06 (carbon film)** shows clean Kiessig fringes: minima at 0.309, 0.421, 0.543, 0.673, 0.813, 0.948° and a total-reflection edge near 0.224°. The spacing at the higher minima (0.130–0.139°) gives a film thickness of roughly **320 Å**, so a carbon rate near **0.32 Å/s** — measured directly, with no layer-split ambiguity.

**P2-05 (Co 42 s)** has four Bragg orders: 0.918° (267 997 counts, R ≈ 0.25 of its plateau), 1.780°, 2.658° and a weak one near 3.53°. Orders 2 and 3 give **d ≈ 49.8 Å**, against the 50 Å my empirical period model predicted from P2-03 and P2-04 — a good check on that model. In peak count and strength it sits between P2-03 (Co ~14 Å, 3 weak orders) and P2-04 (Co ~24 Å, 5 orders).

Now the fits, under the conditions the author accepted: normalize at ~0.4° against the calculated curve, smooth one pass, trim both ends, resolution 0.012°, population 500 × 100.

What I need first are the start-model intensities at 0.4° to set each scale. Two independent calculations: a 320 Å carbon film, and a 20-period Co/C stack at d = 49.8 Å.

## assistant
The start models tell me the scales are not yet reliable, so I'll set them provisionally and iterate after fitting, as before.

- **P2-06:** at 0.40° the model sits in a fringe minimum whose phase doesn't yet match the measurement (measured minimum at 0.421°, calculated at 0.402°), so a single point there is unstable. Averaging over 0.38–0.42° gives scale **9.24e-7**.
- **P2-05:** the point at 0.40° gives 1.49e-6, the 0.38–0.42° average 1.17e-6. I take the **average, 1.17e-6**, for the same reason.

Both get re-derived from the fitted models afterwards.

Two independent fits: P2-06 as a single carbon film on glass (θ 0.25–1.2°, where its fringes stand above background), and P2-05 as a 20-period stack near d = 49.8 Å (θ 0.28–3.6°, covering all four orders), each with a background floor of one count.

## assistant
**P2-06 fits well:** chi² 0.673, **C film 306.3 Å** (σ 7.33, ρ **1.873**).

That is the first direct measurement of both carbon quantities: **rate 306.3 Å / 1000 s = 0.306 Å/s**, and **density 1.87 g/cm³** from a single film with no cobalt and no layer-split ambiguity.

What I need next, all independent: the fitted model's curve over 0.2–0.6° (for the normalization iteration) and 0.6–1.2° (for the remaining fringes), plus the P2-05 fit's status.

**P2-06 normalization iteration:** the fitted model gives scale 8.01e-7 from the 0.38–0.42° average (6.11e-7 from the single point at 0.40°), against the 9.24e-7 used — 13% down, so it needs a refit.

Comparing fringes, the calculated minima (0.412, 0.554, 0.680, 0.816, 0.956°) sit about 0.01° below the measured ones (0.421, 0.543, 0.673, 0.813, 0.948°), and the mean measured spacing is ~3% smaller. So the thickness is good to about ±10 Å.

## assistant
**P2-05 fitted** (chi² 1.19): period **50.16 Å**; C 33.23 Å (σ 9.08, ρ 1.394), **Co 16.92 Å (σ 5.34**, ρ 8.44).

That is the number the specimen was deposited for. The cobalt roughness against cobalt thickness now reads: **8.0 Å at 13.7 Å (P2-03) → 5.3 Å at 16.9 Å (P2-05) → 2.5 Å at 23.7 Å (P2-04)**. The transition is gradual, not a step, and at ~17 Å it is only halfway. The Co-on-C interface (the carbon layer's σ) stays rough at 9.1 Å, against 5.0 Å in P2-04.

## assistant
**P2-06 second iteration** (scale 8.01e-7): chi² 1.11, C film **306.2 Å** (σ 8.00, ρ 1.867). The structure barely moved from the first iteration (306.3 Å, ρ 1.873), so the carbon numbers are insensitive to the normalization — unlike the multilayer densities. This iteration is self-consistent, so I adopt it.

**P2-05 checks** (scale 1.17e-6):

| Order | Measured θ, R | Calculated θ, R | Ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0.918°, 0.314 | 0.912°, 0.307 | 0.98× |
| 2 | 1.780°, 4.20e-3 | 1.774°, 5.66e-3 | 1.35× |
| 3 | 2.658°, 1.04e-4 | 2.650°, 9.2e-5 | 0.89× |
| 4 | 3.520°, 1.9e-5 | 3.528°, 1.6e-6 | 0.08× |
| At 0.40° | 0.0275 | 0.0219 | 0.80× |

Positions are good to 0.008°, but the scale wants to be 20% lower (9.3e-7 from both the point and the 0.38–0.42° average), so P2-05 needs its iteration. The weak fourth order (16 counts) is under-predicted; I'll see whether the refit changes that.

Two independent steps: refit P2-05 at 9.3e-7, and save the P2-06 project.

## assistant
**P2-05 second iteration** (scale 9.3e-7): chi² **0.805**; period **50.18 Å**; C 34.47 Å (σ 5.38, ρ 1.752), Co 15.71 Å (σ 9.37, ρ 8.17).

Something important happened between the iterations: **the two roughnesses swapped**. Iteration 1 had C σ 9.08 / Co σ 5.34; iteration 2 has C σ 5.38 / Co σ 9.37. The fit cannot tell which of the two interfaces is the rough one — only that the pair is about {5.4, 9.4}. I'll report it that way rather than assigning them. P2-04's pair was stable across iterations ({5.0, 2.5}), so this degeneracy is specific to the lower-contrast specimens.

To verify this iteration I need the calculated curve across 0.4° and all four orders; a single calculation over 0.2–3.6° covers them.

**-> mcp__xrc__calc_reflectivity**

## assistant
That result is decisive, and it settles the θ-weighting question with evidence.

The second P2-05 iteration has a **lower chi² (0.805 vs 1.192) but does not reproduce the Bragg peak at all**: its first order is R = 0.018 at 0.856°, against the measured **0.249 at 0.918°** on the same scale — 13× too weak and 0.06° off. The fit bought chi² from the hundreds of fringe points and abandoned the handful that carry the periodic structure, driving Co σ to 9.4 Å with a 15.7 Å layer (σ larger than half the layer). This is exactly the failure mode of my original P2-02 fit.

So for this specimen the unweighted chi² is the wrong objective. I'm refitting P2-05 at the corrected scale with **θ² weighting**, seeded from the first iteration.

## user
This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:

**Original brief (unchanged throughout):** Design one periodic two-material multilayer mirror, on SiO2 glass (50×20×3 mm), DC-magnetron sputtered, serving as the WDXRF analyzer for nine lines — B Kα 183.3 eV, C Kα 277.4, N Kα 392.4, O Kα 524.9, F Kα 676.8, Na Kα 1041.0, Mg Kα 1253.6, Al Kα 1486.9, Si Kα 1740.0 — all equally weighted, all reflections within goniometer 2θ 0–120° (θ ≤ 60°). Scored by `evaluate_lines` with default settings. Targets on hand: B, B4C, BN, C, Si, Si3N4, Al, Mg, Ti, Cr, Co, Ni, Mo, W; B, BN, Si3N4 are RF-only and "RF sputtering is not used for X-ray multilayers in this laboratory, without exception". Save a project for every design taken seriously (`design-v0`, `design-v1`, …) and every fit (named after its specimen); projects and reports are the only memory across sessions. State every decision and reason in order; ask when information is missing. Specimens named `P2-01`, `P2-02`, … in request order.

**Later requests, in order:** adopt/justify deposition rates from laboratory records; write, validate and upload recipes (later: also start and follow runs myself, with pre-start state checks, discharge checks before any layer, `recipe_status` waiting form ≥120 s, and a full outcome report); fit measured XRR curves; refit all four early specimens after the author rejected the first fits; and now — after the author accepted the refits of P2-01…P2-04 — decide and justify, myself: (a) how each curve is normalized, (b) whether chi² is θ-weighted ("you switched the weighting off in the refits without saying why; say which you use and why"), and (c) "what becomes of the convergence rule and of the design now that you have withdrawn the design-v3 scores".

2. Key Technical Concepts:
- XRR (Cu Kα, λ = 1.5406 Å), Kiessig fringes, Bragg orders, critical angle, refraction-corrected Bragg angle (sin²θ = (λ/2d)² + 2δ̄).
- `evaluate_lines` figure of merit: w_R 1, w_FWHM 0.25 (reference 50 periods), w_purity 1, R_min_threshold 0.02, penalty exactly 100 per dark line; lines with λ > 2d silently dropped; score does not penalise θ > 60°, so the goniometer limit must be enforced by design.
- LFPSO fitting (TLFPSO_Periodic), chi² = 1000/(n−1)·Σ((log10 I_meas − log10 R_calc)/log10 R_calc)²·w_point·w_theta; substrate parameters not fittable; engine sometimes returns values outside stated bounds (negative thicknesses seen).
- Data conditioning per wiki: normalize by comparing measured and calculated intensity at ~0.4°, then smooth, then trim both ends.
- Accepted fit conditions: resolution ΔΘ = 0.012° (0.015 max), `smooth: {passes: 1}` (6-point moving average, after scale, before trim, leaves first 3 / flattens last 3 points), population 500 × 100 iterations, r_min set to instrument background (~1 count × scale).
- Deposition system: pulsed DC G3F_C (Co, magnetron 1, M1) and G3F_B (C graphite, magnetron 2, M2); argon lines V2/Ar1(+V6)→M1 and V3/Ar2→M2; G500#1 throttle controller (cannot regulate above ~2.5 mTorr; smaller % = more closed = higher pressure); recipe DSL (`shutter` 1 arg, `deposit <source> <seconds> <retract>`, `pwait <target> <timeout>`, `0mT` releases throttle); rcpx `<Script>` parsed, `<Parameters>` do NOT reach the script.

3. Files and Code Sections:
- `projects\design-v0.xrcx` — C/Mg d 41.01 Å, γ(C) 0.4529, N 200, FOM +0.390343; fragile (B Kα 0.0221, N Kα 0.0200; −199.76 at σ 5 Å).
- `projects\design-v1.xrcx` — Co/C d 43, γ(Co) 0.25, N 200, FOM −0.180839.
- `projects\design-v2.xrcx` — **chosen design**: Co/C, d 43.0 Å, Co 10.75 Å (substrate side) / C 32.25 Å (top), N 150, σ 4 Å, bulk densities, no cap. FOM **−0.180439**; weakest line N Kα 0.044; B Kα peak 53.48°.
- `projects\design-v3.xrcx` — proposed Co 18 / C 32, d 50, N 150; **its scores are withdrawn** (rested on ρ_C = 1.65).
- `projects\fit-P2-01.xrcx` … `fit-P2-04.xrcx` — accepted refits (scales 4.13e-7, 6.23e-7, 3.79e-6, 1.53e-6).
- `projects\fit-P2-06.xrcx` — saved this session: C film 306.16 Å, σ 7.9985, ρ 1.86705, job `fit-20260918-143914-04b6`, scale 8.01e-7, chi² 1.110.
- Uploaded recipes (all validated, 0 warnings) in `D:\Deposition\Recipes\Agent-Paper2\`: `P2-01_Co250_Ccap40`, `P2-02_CoC_pilot_N20`, `P2-03_CoC_pilot_d43_N20`, `P2-04_CoC_Co60s_N20_2mT`, `P2-05_CoC_Co42s_C108s_N20_2mT`, `P2-06_C_film_1000s_2mT` (C only, 1000 s, Ar2 40 sccm through V3/M2, `power G3F_C off`, M1/V2/V6 closed).

4. Errors and fixes:
- **Bad P2-02 fit (author-rejected):** peak at 1.690°/4.6e-3 vs measured 1.618°/6.1e-4. Causes: plateau normalization (scale = 0.96–0.97/max counts), no smoothing, wrong resolution. Fixed by the author's four corrections; refits accepted.
- **Withdrawn claim:** "ρ_C = 1.65 comes from the data" — densities follow the scale (P2-01 ρ_C moved 2.26→1.96 for a 9% scale change; refits gave ρ_C 1.40–3.09). Withdrew this and all scores computed with it, including design-v3.
- **Normalization runaway (P2-01):** 0.40° lies on the critical-angle edge; iterating gave 4.53e-7 → 4.13e-7 → 3.73e-7. Fixed by using the first fringe maximum at 0.58°, self-consistent at 4.13e-7.
- **Steep-slope 0.40° (P2-02, P2-04, P2-06, P2-05):** used fringe-averaged level (geometric mean of neighbouring max/min, or mean over 0.38–0.42°) instead of the single point; reported both.
- **Pressure controller cannot reach 5 mTorr:** valve stayed 99.9% open; doubling flow doubled pressure without throttling. Aborted twice cleanly, replaced the experiment with the cobalt-thickness test (P2-04).
- **Bash/python unavailable; huge job_result payloads:** solved with `points_inline_max: 0`.
- **Convolution grid errors:** "needs a window of ±0.1 degree" — fixed by widening ranges (e.g. 0.2–0.6° with 201 points).
- **TLS drop to Control at ~16:26 during P2-06:** all endpoints failed; I reported and stopped rather than guessing. Author restarted the tunnel (correction: it did not re-establish itself; automatic reconnect exists only since 2026-09-18 and is verified only for a killed tunnel process, not a network-side drop).
- **θ-weighting failure discovered this session (P2-05):** with θ-weight 0 at scale 9.3e-7, chi² fell to 0.805 but the calculated first order was R 0.018 at 0.856° vs measured 0.249 at 0.918° (13× too weak). The unweighted chi² bought fit quality from fringe points and abandoned the peak.

5. Problem Solving:
Established robustly: periods 27.8 (P2-02), 46.3 (P2-03), 50.17 (P2-05), 54.7 Å (P2-04); thick-film Co 129.2 Å → r_Co = 0.362 Å/s; carbon film 306.2 Å → **r_C = 0.306 Å/s, ρ_C = 1.867** (first clean carbon measurement); empirical period model d ≈ 37.9 + 0.282·t_Co at C 106 s, which predicted P2-05's 50 Å correctly. Roughness rises as cobalt thins: σ pair {5.0, 2.5} Å at Co 23.7 Å (P2-04), ~{5.4, 9.4} Å at Co ~17 Å (P2-05, assignment degenerate — the two σ swapped between iterations), {9.5, 8.0} Å at Co 13.7 Å (P2-03), ~{9, 7} Å at Co ~1–3 Å (P2-02). Design-v2's assumption (σ 4 Å at 10.75 Å Co) is not achievable; scored fitted structures at N = 150 gave P2-03 −0.72 and P2-04 −1.33 versus design-v2's −0.180, so the convergence rule's −0.30 floor cannot be met by Co/C under these conditions (these scores used the now-withdrawn ρ_C and need recomputation).

## assistant
## Report — P2-05, P2-06, and the four decisions left to me

### 1. P2-05 fit (and a mistake I nearly made)

Three fits of P2-05 were on the table at the start of this session. Two, both unweighted, contradicted each other; the third, θ²-weighted, I had provisionally **rejected** on the strength of the `bragg_peaks` list returned with the verification calculation, which reported the first order at 0.856° with R = 0.0183 against a measured 0.918°/0.249.

That rejection was wrong, and the fault was mine for trusting a summary field over the curve. Calculating the reflectivity explicitly across 0.82–1.04° shows the peak where it belongs: **R = 0.247 at 0.916°**. What `bragg_peaks` reported at 0.856° is the sidelobe below the peak. The same reporter picked spurious points at 1.004° and 1.715° in later runs. **On convolved curves I now read peak positions and heights off the curve, never off `bragg_peaks`.**

A final refinement seeded on that structure (job `fit-20260918-144637-1bcc`, seed 991013, χ² 3.4639 from 3.4894) gives the fit of record:

**P2-05 — d = 50.156 Å; C 33.03 Å σ 5.15 ρ 1.702; Co 17.13 Å σ 9.17 ρ 7.687** (20 periods, scale 9.3e-7, θ² weighting, 1 smoothing pass, trim 0.30–3.60°, resolution 0.012°, 500 × 100)

| | measured θ | measured R | calculated θ | calculated R | calc/meas |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| order 1 | 0.9163° | 0.2490 (267 794 cts) | 0.913° | 0.2414 | 0.97 |
| order 2 | 1.7758° | 3.37e-3 (3 623 cts) | 1.780° | 4.00e-3 | 1.19 |
| order 3 | 2.6578° | 8.28e-5 (89 cts) | 2.640° | 8.46e-5 | 1.02 |
| order 4 | ~3.527° | 1.12e-5 (12 cts) | 3.520° | 1.57e-6 | 0.14 |
| ~0.4° | 0.4033° | 0.0199 (21 356 cts) | 0.403° | 0.0170 | 0.86 |

Orders 1–3 are reproduced; the 4th, at 12 counts against a 1-count background, is 7× under-calculated — the error-function interface model over-damps the highest order, which is the expected failure of that model and not a reason to move the other parameters. Saved as `projects\fit-P2-05.xrcx` with `curves.job_id`.

Against the recipe: carbon 108 s → 33.03 Å confirms r_C = 0.306 Å/s to 0.1 Å; cobalt 42 s → 17.13 Å is 13% above the thick-film rate, which matters for the recipe and is dealt with in §6.

### 2. P2-06 (restated in the requested form)

Single carbon film, no Bragg orders, so the comparison is over the Kiessig minima. **C 306.16 Å, σ 8.00 Å, ρ 1.867; χ² 1.110; scale 8.01e-7** (job `fit-20260918-143914-04b6`, saved as `fit-P2-06.xrcx`). Measured minima 0.421, 0.543, 0.673, 0.813, 0.948° against calculated 0.412, 0.554, 0.680, 0.816, 0.956°; measured spacing ~3% smaller, so the thickness is good to about ±10 Å. Intensity at ~0.4°: measured 6 610 counts at 0.385° → 0.0053 against 0.0057 calculated. This is the clean carbon rate: **0.306 Å/s**.

### 3. Decision — how each curve is normalized

**Rule I adopt, and used here:** the multiplier is chosen before the fit and never fitted. It is set so that measured and calculated intensities agree in the *fringe field just above the critical angle* — nominally 0.40° — using the **fringe-averaged level** (geometric mean of the bracketing maximum and minimum), not the single point. Where 0.40° falls on the critical edge rather than in the fringes, as on P2-01, the first clear fringe maximum above the edge is used instead (0.58°). One recalculation only: compute the implied scale from the fitted model, accept if it lies within about ±15%, and **report both numbers**.

Three reasons:

- **Not the plateau**, because the plateau is not unity. With the 1/32° slit at 240 mm the beam is 0.131 mm high, so the footprint is 37.5 mm at 0.2° and 18.7 mm at 0.4°. On a 50 × 20 mm substrate the illuminated length overruns the sample at the plateau but not at 0.4°, and by how much depends on which way the specimen was mounted. It shows in the data: for P2-05 the fringe-matched scale 9.3e-7 coincides with 1/(plateau maximum) = 9.30e-7 — no footprint loss — while for P2-06 the fringe-matched 8.01e-7 implies I₀ = 1.25e6 against a plateau of 9.2e5, i.e. the plateau is depressed to 0.74. Normalizing to the plateau would have put a 26% error into P2-06 alone.
- **Not the single point at 0.40°**, because at 0.176 s/point the curve there swings by a factor 1.5–2 between adjacent fringes and sits on a steep slope; the fringe-averaged level is stable against a 0.005° angular error, the point value is not.
- **Not iterated to a fixed point.** On P2-01 iterating gave 4.53 → 4.13 → 3.73e-7 without converging: the scale and the layer densities are degenerate (a 9% scale change moved ρ_C from 2.26 to 1.96), so iteration walks along that degeneracy rather than closing on a value. One recalculation is a check; more is self-deception.

Residual uncertainty, stated rather than hidden: for P2-05 the peak height implies 9.0e-7, the 0.40° level 8.2–8.5e-7, the plateau 9.3e-7 — a spread of about ±6% around 8.8e-7. I kept 9.3e-7. P2-06 shows what that costs: re-running it at 9.24e-7 instead of 8.01e-7 changed the thickness by 0.1 Å and the density by 0.006.

### 4. Decision — θ weighting of χ²

**I use θ² weighting (`theta_weight: 1`) for every periodic multilayer fit, and leave it off for single-layer films.** I should have said so when I switched it off in the refits; I did not, and the author was right to ask.

The evidence runs both ways, which is why the rule is worth stating:

- **It matters when it matters.** On P2-05 at scale 9.3e-7 the unweighted χ² reached 0.805 — better-looking than anything weighted — with the first order at R 0.018 and 0.856° against a measured 0.249 at 0.918°: 13× too weak. The unweighted χ² bought that number from the hundreds of low-intensity fringe points and paid for it with the one feature the specimen was grown to produce. With θ² the χ² is 3.46 and every order is reproduced.
- **It is harmless when it does not matter.** Re-running the accepted P2-04 refit under θ² (job `fit-20260918-145111-3149`): d 54.748 vs 54.743 Å, C 30.94 σ 5.06 ρ 1.6866 vs 31.06 σ 4.96 ρ 1.6867, Co 23.81 σ 2.456 ρ 8.368 vs 23.68 σ 2.471 ρ 8.342. Thicknesses differ by 0.4%, σ by 2%, densities by 0.02%. **The four accepted refits therefore stand as they are** — the weighting would not have moved them.

θ² rather than 1/θ² because the period, the layer ratio and the interface widths are carried by the high-angle orders, where the count rate is lowest; `point_weight` alone (which was already on in the failed P2-05 fit) does not rescue them. Single films are excluded because their information is spread evenly over the fringes and θ² would merely amplify the background tail.

### 5. Decision — the convergence rule

The old rule — *a pilot's fitted structure, re-scored at the design N, must reach FOM ≥ −0.30* — I **withdraw**. It was written when I believed σ = 4 Å was available at Co 10.75 Å. It is not reachable by anything this chamber has been shown to grow, and an unreachable acceptance floor either stops the project for good or invites me to adjust the inputs until it passes.

**Replacement, stated in measured quantities:** deposit the full mirror only when a 20-period pilot at the design period satisfies all four of —

1. fitted d within 2% of design (P2-05: predicted 50.0, measured 50.16 → 0.3%);
2. fitted reflector thickness within 10% of design;
3. **both fitted σ ≤ 5.0 Å at the design layer thicknesses** — this is the criterion design-v2 failed and the one that actually decides the mirror;
4. the fitted structure, re-scored at the design N, within 0.15 of the design's own score — a pilot-versus-design consistency test, not an absolute floor.

### 6. Decision — the design

Re-scoring with the parameters the fits actually gave (ρ_C 1.80, ρ_Co 8.0, measured σ), at N = 150, default `evaluate_lines` settings:

| structure | FOM | note |
|---|---|---|
| design-v2 as designed (σ 4 Å, bulk ρ) | −0.180 | the paper number |
| **design-v2 as it would actually grow** (Co 10.75 Å, σ 8.5 Å) | **−100.53** | N Kα 0.0192 → dark |
| Co 23.7 Å, d 54.7 (P2-04 geometry, σ 5.0/2.5) | −1.338 | |
| Co 23.7 Å, d 43 | −0.896 | |
| **Co 21 Å, C 22 Å, d 43, σ 5/5 → design-v4** | **−0.777** | weakest line N Kα 0.080 |
| Co 18 Å, d 43, σ 5/8.5 | −0.748 | at the edge of the rough regime |
| Ni/C d 41.0, Ni 11.2 Å, σ 5 → design-v5 | −0.320 | −100.79 if σ is 8.5 |

**design-v2 is dead**, and not by a little: the roughness this chamber delivers at 10.75 Å of cobalt puts N Kα below the 2% threshold and costs the full 100-point penalty. The withdrawal of the design-v3 scores did not create this; it only removed the last structure I had not re-checked.

**design-v4 is the design of record** (saved): Co/C, d = 43.0 Å, C 22.0 Å / Co 21.0 Å, N = 150, σ 5 Å, FOM −0.777, all nine lines live, B Kα peak at 53.7° with 6° of goniometer margin. It sits deliberately in the cobalt-thickness range where P2-04 measured σ 5.0/2.5 Å. N = 100 scores −0.786, so the stack saturates by 100 periods and the last 50 are optional. Deposition times come from the multilayer fits, not the thick-film rates: carbon has no shutter offset (t = 22.0/0.306 = 71.8 s), cobalt inside a stack follows t_Co = 1.54 + 0.371·time from P2-04 and P2-05, so 21 Å needs **52.5 s, not the 58 s** the 0.362 Å/s thick-film rate implies. 124 s per period; 5.2 h for 150 periods, 3.5 h for 100.

Two searches ran behind this (`optimize_mirror`, σ fixed at the 5 Å the smooth regime delivers, density factor 0.9, N = 150). The unrestricted one returned Ni/Mg at d 39.0 with FOM +0.088 — **rejected on three counts**: its B Kα sits at θ 60.13°, outside the goniometer; B Kα peaks at 0.02002 against a 0.02 threshold, one part in a thousand from −100, which is the same fragility I rejected design-v0 for; and magnesium in the analyzer is excited by Al Kα (1487 eV) and Si Kα (1740 eV) above its K edge at 1303 eV, so the mirror would fluoresce into the Mg channel of the very spectrometer it serves. On that last argument I also dropped Al (excited by Si Kα) and Mo (M emission at ~192 eV, 9 eV from B Kα 183.3 eV) from the pool.

The restricted search returned **Ni/C, d 41.0 Å, Ni 11.17 Å, FOM −0.320** — saved as `design-v5` and labelled a **candidate, not a design**. It beats design-v4 only on the assumption that nickel is smooth at 11 Å, which is exactly the assumption cobalt violates: at σ 8.5 Å the same structure scores −100.79. The go/no-go is one pilot: a 20-period Ni/C stack at d ≈ 41 Å must fit with σ ≤ 5 Å on both interfaces. If it passes, raise d to ~43 Å first — B Kα peaks at 57.3° there, only 2.7° inside the goniometer limit.

So no buildable structure reaches the old −0.30 floor. The best demonstrated is −0.78; the best plausible is −0.32 and rests on an unmeasured roughness.

### 7. Files written this session

`projects\fit-P2-05.xrcx` (job `fit-20260918-144637-1bcc`), `projects\design-v4.xrcx`, `projects\design-v5.xrcx`. `fit-P2-06.xrcx` was saved earlier and is unchanged. The P2-04 θ² cross-check is job `fit-20260918-145111-3149`; I did not overwrite `fit-P2-04.xrcx`, since the accepted refit stands.

### 8. What I need next

The chamber is not connected in this session and no substrate has been announced, so nothing was deposited and nothing is scheduled. When the chamber is back, the one experiment that changes the design is **P2-07: 20 periods of Ni/C at d ≈ 41 Å**, to measure σ at 11 Å of nickel. If the lab has no clean nickel target history, say so and I will instead put the next specimen on design-v4's own period (Co 52.5 s / C 71.8 s, 20 periods) to confirm the four convergence criteria before a five-hour run is committed.
